## GHSA-c94f-87xg-65wf · silverstripe/framework

2026-11-16 · PHP · **HIGH** 8.8 · Pattern: `DESERIALIZATION→RCE`

### Root cause

The `i18nTextCollectorTask` allowed an attacker to specify an arbitrary class name for the 'writer' option via a GET parameter. The application would then instantiate this class using `Injector::inst()->get()`, which could lead to arbitrary object instantiation and method calls if a malicious class was provided, ultimately resulting in RCE.

### Impact

An unauthenticated attacker could execute arbitrary code on the server, leading to full system compromise.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Dev/Tasks/i18nTextCollectorTask.php
+++ b/src/Dev/Tasks/i18nTextCollectorTask.php
@@ -36,6 +38,9 @@ protected function execute(InputInterface $input, PolyOutput $output): int
         // Custom writer
         $writerName = $input->getOption('writer');
         if ($writerName) {
+            if (!is_a($writerName, Writer::class, true)) {
+                throw new InvalidArgumentException('The writer option must be the name of a class that implements ' . Writer::class);
+            }
             $writer = Injector::inst()->get($writerName);
             $collector->setWriter($writer);
         }
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds a validation check to the `i18nTextCollectorTask`'s 'writer' option. It now verifies that the provided class name implements the `SilverStripe\i18n\Messages\Writer` interface before attempting to instantiate it, preventing the instantiation of arbitrary classes.

## GHSA-28qf-cpwq-vmc2 · aiohttp

2026-11-16 · Python · **HIGH** 8.2 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an incomplete fix for a prior HTTP request smuggling issue. When an HTTP upgrade (e.g., to WebSockets) was declined, the pure-Python HTTP parser would continue to process incoming data, buffering subsequent requests without proper flow control. This allowed an attacker to send a large number of small requests, causing the server to buffer an unbounded amount of data.

### Impact

An attacker could exhaust the server's memory resources by continuously sending requests that are buffered but not processed, leading to a denial of service for legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/aiohttp/web_protocol.py
+++ b/aiohttp/web_protocol.py
@@ -774,6 +774,12 @@ async def finish_response(
                 for msg, payload in messages:
                     self._request_count += 1
                     self._messages.append((msg, payload))
+                if (
+                    not self._msg_queue_paused
+                    and len(self._messages) >= self._max_msg_queue_size
+                ):
+                    self._pause_msg_queue_reading()
                 assert self._waiter is None

--- aiohttp/http_parser.py
+++ aiohttp/http_parser.py
@@ -371,6 +371,8 @@ def feed_data(
                     # any preceding body is consumed before the next request
                     # line. Resumes via feed_data(b"") when the queue drains.
                     self._tail = data[start_pos:]
+                    data = EMPTY
                     break
                 pos = data.find(SEP, start_pos)
                 # consume \r\n
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a mechanism to pause the transport when the message queue reaches a predefined maximum size, similar to how data_received() handles flow control. It also ensures that any remaining data after a request is parsed is explicitly handled as part of the internal buffer and not returned for further processing, preventing re-feeding of data.

## GHSA-w553-pwx6-3mg9 · litesaml/lightsaml

2026-11-16 · PHP · **HIGH** 8.1 · Pattern: `MISSING_VERIFICATION→SIGNATURE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the XML signature validation process did not adequately verify that the signed element was the intended one. An attacker could craft an XML document with duplicate IDs, where a legitimate signed element was present but a forged element with the same ID was processed by the application, effectively bypassing signature validation.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass the integrity checks of signed XML documents, allowing them to inject malicious data or alter the content of SAML assertions without invalidating the signature. This could lead to unauthorized access, privilege escalation, or other security breaches depending on the application's use of SAML.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Model/XmlDSig/SignatureXmlReader.php
+++ b/src/Model/XmlDSig/SignatureXmlReader.php
@@ -55,6 +55,9 @@ public function validate(XMLSecurityKey $key): bool
             return false;
         }
 
+        // Must run before validateReference() because that call detaches sigNode from the document.
+        $this->assertNoXmlSignatureWrapping();
+
         try {
             $this->signature->validateReference();
         } catch (Exception $e) {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new validation step, `assertNoXmlSignatureWrapping()`, which runs before the standard reference validation. This new method verifies that each fragment reference URI points to the direct parent of the signature and that the referenced ID is unique within the document. It explicitly checks for duplicate IDs, which are a prerequisite for XML Signature Wrapping attacks.

## GHSA-7mxv-7h3q-9324 · nltk

2026-11-16 · Python · **HIGH** 7.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The application executed the Graphviz 'dot' binary using `subprocess.run` without proper path sanitization or environment scrubbing. This allowed an attacker with local write access to a directory in the system's PATH environment variable to place a malicious executable named 'dot', which would then be executed instead of the legitimate Graphviz binary.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve local code execution on the system where the NLTK library is used, potentially leading to full system compromise if the application runs with elevated privileges.

### Key diff

```
--- proc = subprocess.run(
+++ proc = spawn_trusted(
     dot_binary,
     ["-T%s" % t],
     stdin=subprocess.PIPE,
```

### Fix summary

The patch replaces `subprocess.run` with `nltk.pathsec.spawn_trusted`. This new function acts as a trusted execution chokepoint, verifying that the binary is located on a secure path, refusing a shell, and scrubbing the loader environment before execution, thereby mitigating path-based attacks.

## GHSA-24c4-3p7v-29cm · aiohttp

2026-11-16 · Python · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→INFINITE_LOOP`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from how aiohttp's multipart parser handled base64 encoded parts. When processing base64 data, it would strip whitespace and then check for a remainder of 0 when dividing by 4. If the remainder was not 0, it would attempt to read more data to complete the base64 quartet. However, if the `client_max_size` limit was reached, the `_content.read()` call would return an empty byte string, leading to an infinite loop as the `remainder` would never become 0 and `at_eof()` would never be true, effectively hanging the server.

### Impact

An attacker could send a specially crafted multipart request with a base64 encoded part that causes the server to enter an infinite loop, leading to a denial of service. This could make the server unresponsive and unavailable to legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/aiohttp/multipart.py
+++ b/aiohttp/multipart.py
@@ -358,43 +366,59 @@ async def read_chunk(self, size: int = chunk_size) -> bytes:
         if encoding and encoding.lower() == "base64":
-            stripped_chunk = b"".join(chunk.split())
-            remainder = len(stripped_chunk) % 4
-
-            while remainder != 0 and not self.at_eof():
-                over_chunk_size = 4 - remainder
-                over_chunk = b""
-
-                if self._prev_chunk:
-                    over_chunk = self._prev_chunk[:over_chunk_size]
-                    self._prev_chunk = self._prev_chunk[len(over_chunk) :]
-
-                if len(over_chunk) != over_chunk_size:
-                    over_chunk += await self._content.read(4 - len(over_chunk))
-
-                if not over_chunk:
-                    self._at_eof = True
-
-                stripped_chunk += b"".join(over_chunk.split())
-                chunk += over_chunk
-                remainder = len(stripped_chunk) % 4
+            chunk = self._align_base64_chunk(chunk, len(carry) + want)
 
         if self._read_bytes == self._length:
             self._at_eof = True
         if self._at_eof and await self._content.readline() != b"\r\n":
             raise ValueError("Reader did not read all the data or it is malformed")
         return chunk
+
+    def _align_base64_chunk(self, chunk: bytes, size: int) -> bytes:
+        at_end = self._at_eof or (
+            self._length is not None and self._read_bytes >= self._length
+        )
+        if not at_end and len(chunk) > size:
+            self._b64_carry = chunk[size:]
+            chunk = chunk[:size]
+
+        remainder = len(chunk.translate(None, _NON_BASE64_BYTES)) % 4
+        if not remainder or at_end:
+            return chunk
+
+        # Walk back over the trailing partial quartet and carry it into the
+        # next chunk.
+        cut = len(chunk)
+        left = remainder
+        while left:
+            cut -= 1
+            if chunk[cut] in _BASE64_CHARS:
+                left -= 1
+        if not cut:
+            # No whole quartet to hand back, and carrying the lot would make
+            # no progress: the caller asked for this many bytes, and a part
+            # that holds no quartet within them holds none to give.
+            return chunk
+
+        self._b64_carry = chunk[cut:] + self._b64_carry
+        return chunk[:cut]
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `_align_base64_chunk` method to correctly handle base64 chunk alignment. It now carries over partial base64 quartets to the next chunk instead of attempting to read more data when the `client_max_size` limit might be reached. It also defines `_BASE64_CHARS` and `_NON_BASE64_BYTES` for more robust base64 character validation.

## GHSA-3r8g-jcqq-3p5j · @sveltejs/kit

2026-11-16 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The previous implementation of processing FormData in SvelteKit used `data.keys()` to iterate over field names and then `data.getAll(field_name)` for each name. This approach has a quadratic time complexity (O(n^2)) with respect to the number of form entries, as `getAll` itself iterates through all entries. For a large number of form fields, this led to significant processing delays.

### Impact

An attacker could send a specially crafted form with a large number of fields to a SvelteKit application, causing the server to spend excessive time processing the form data. This could lead to a denial of service, making the application unresponsive to legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/kit/src/runtime/form-utils.js
+++ b/packages/kit/src/runtime/form-utils.js
 	const result = {};
 
-	for (const field_name of data.keys()) {
-		/** @type {any[]} */
-		const values = data.getAll(field_name);
+	for (const [field_name, values] of group_formdata(data)) {
 
 		const field = parse_form_key(form_id, field_name);
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new utility function `group_formdata` that processes FormData entries in a single linear pass (O(n)) to group values by name. This optimized grouping is then used in `convert_formdata` and `handle_issues` to replace the inefficient `data.getAll(field_name)` calls, reducing the time complexity from quadratic to linear.

## GHSA-3rp8-rxj8-mvjw · aiohttp

2026-11-16 · Python · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `INTEGER_OVERFLOW→BOUNDARY`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an integer overflow in the calculation of `projected_size` when checking against `_max_msg_size`. When `self._payload_bytes_to_read` was a large negative number (due to being a signed 64-bit C value) and `len(self._partial)` was a positive number, their sum could wrap around, resulting in a small positive `projected_size` that bypassed the `_max_msg_size` check.

### Impact

An attacker could send a WebSocket message larger than the configured `_max_msg_size` limit, potentially leading to denial of service (resource exhaustion) or other unexpected behavior in the server application.

### Key diff

```
-                    projected_size = self._payload_bytes_to_read + len(self._partial)
-                    if projected_size >= self._max_msg_size:
+                    partial_len = len(self._partial)
+                    if self._payload_bytes_to_read >= self._max_msg_size - partial_len:
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the integer overflow by changing the order of operations in the size check. Instead of adding `_payload_bytes_to_read` and `len(self._partial)` and then comparing, it now subtracts `len(self._partial)` from `_max_msg_size` and then compares `_payload_bytes_to_read` against this adjusted limit, preventing the overflow.

## GHSA-72px-j8gh-p79x · mail-parser

2026-11-16 · Python · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→HEADER`

### Root cause

The `mail-parser` library's `get_server_ipaddress()` function and other header parsing logic was vulnerable to sender-controlled input. Specifically, it performed attribute lookups and name rewriting on sender-provided header names, allowing attackers to inject specially crafted headers to either spoof the sender's IP address or trigger resource exhaustion (e.g., infinite loops, excessive memory usage) due to quadratic complexity in header processing or recursive attribute access.

### Impact

An attacker could spoof the sender's IP address, leading to misattribution of email origin. Additionally, by crafting malicious headers, an attacker could trigger a denial of service (DoS) against the mail-parser application, causing it to consume excessive CPU or memory, or crash due to stack exhaustion.

### Key diff

```
-            value = getattr(self, i)
+            if i in _RESERVED_MAIL_KEYS:
+                continue
+            value = getattr(self, i) if i in COMPUTED_PARTS else self._header_value(i)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a header index (`_header_index`) to optimize header lookups and prevent quadratic complexity. It also explicitly separates sender-chosen header names from internal computed parts and reserved keys, ensuring that attribute access for headers goes through a controlled `_header_value()` method that performs no attribute lookup or name rewriting, thus preventing malicious header names from interfering with internal object attributes or triggering recursive calls.

## GHSA-h4cc-hm5h-fmm9 · aiohttp

2026-11-16 · Python · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The previous implementation of the `ZLibDecompressor` handled concatenated compressed streams inefficiently. When multiple small members were concatenated, the decompressor would repeatedly create new decompressor objects and copy `unused_data`, leading to quadratic time complexity and excessive CPU usage.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious compressed stream with many small concatenated members, causing the server to consume excessive CPU resources during decompression, leading to a denial of service for legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
-        while self._decompressor.eof and self._decompressor.unused_data:
-            unused = self._decompressor.unused_data
-            self._decompressor = self._zlib_backend.decompressobj(wbits=self._mode)
-            # ... (rest of the loop)
+        if self._decompressor.eof and self._decompressor.unused_data:
+            result = self._decompress_members(result, max_length)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `ConcatDecompressionHandler` base class to manage concatenated members more efficiently. It implements a windowing mechanism that starts small and doubles, avoiding quadratic complexity. It also adds a `MAX_DECOMPRESS_MEMBERS` cap to limit the number of members processed in a single call, preventing unbounded resource consumption.

## GHSA-m9j8-jcmv-hp69 · @sveltejs/kit

2026-11-16 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `INFO_DISCLOSURE→STACK_TRACE`

### Root cause

The build-time guard in SvelteKit, designed to prevent server-only modules from being included in client bundles, failed to properly account for module IDs containing query parameters with slashes. Specifically, the `server_only_module_pattern` regex did not match such IDs, and the `is_server_only_path` function was not stripping query parameters before evaluation, leading to server secrets being statically replaced in the client bundle.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially gain access to server-side secrets (e.g., API keys, database credentials) that were intended to remain server-only, by inspecting the client-side JavaScript bundle.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/kit/src/exports/vite/plugins/guard.js
+++ b/packages/kit/src/exports/vite/plugins/guard.js
@@ -103,6 +106,8 @@ export function plugin_guard(kit, get_config, get_manifest_data) {
 					exactRegex(app_server),
 					exactRegex(app_env_private),
 					server_only_module_pattern,
+					// `server_only_module_pattern` won't match if the query contains a `/`
+					server_only_module_with_query_pattern,
 					server_only_directory_pattern
 				]
 			},
@@ -112,7 +117,7 @@ export function plugin_guard(kit, get_config, get_manifest_data) {
 				const is_server_only =
 					normalized === '$app/env/private' ||
 					normalized === '$app/server' ||
-					is_server_only_path(id, {
+					is_server_only_path(id.replace(query_pattern, ''), {
 						root: normalized_cwd,
 						node_modules: normalized_node_modules,
 						routes: normalized_routes,
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new regex `server_only_module_with_query_pattern` to specifically match server-only modules that include query parameters. It also modifies the `is_server_only_path` call to strip query parameters from the module ID before checking if it's a server-only path. Additionally, param matchers are now correctly identified as client entrypoints.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 8 occurrences**
> Languages: PHP, JavaScript, Go, Python
> → GHSA-qrch-52m5-vv85
> → GHSA-9g8x-92q2-p28f
> → GHSA-8h84-fhqq-q58v
> → GHSA-6vj9-mwq6-2f5v
> → GHSA-h85j-hv3c-qfgq
> → GHSA-v3ph-cgqh-r8p5
> → GHSA-c6hw-5grf-ff96

🔗 [View commit](https://github.com/sveltejs/kit/commit/ae07803806f75b8b5662ae4ab92934ca1356fd84)

## GHSA-8vf2-hxg5-crqh · github.com/kubeshark/kubeshark

2026-11-16 · Go · **HIGH** 7.2 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The `pod_regex` argument to `start_kubeshark` was directly appended to the command arguments without proper sanitization or separation, allowing an attacker to inject arbitrary flags. Additionally, the `download_file` function allowed an attacker to specify a destination path without sufficient validation, leading to path traversal.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary file write on the system running the MCP, potentially leading to remote code execution or cluster takeover by writing to sensitive files like `~/.ssh/authorized_keys` or `~/.kube/config`. The argument injection could also allow an attacker to manipulate the `kubeshark tap` command's behavior.

### Key diff

```
--- a/cmd/mcpRunner.go
+++ b/cmd/mcpRunner.go
@@ -941,10 +941,17 @@ func (s *mcpServer) callStartKubeshark(args map[string]any) (string, bool) {
 	// Build the kubeshark tap command
 	cmdArgs := []string{"tap"}
 
-	// Add pod regex if provided
-	if v, ok := args["pod_regex"].(string); ok && v != "" {
-		cmdArgs = append(cmdArgs, v)
-	}
+	// Capture the caller-supplied pod regex. It is appended LAST, after a "--"
+	// end-of-options separator (see below), so a value beginning with "-"
+	// (e.g. "--set=tap.docker.registry=...") can never be parsed as a flag by
+	// the 'tap' cobra command. See CWE-88 (argument injection).
+	podRegex, _ := args["pod_regex"].(string)
 
 	// Add namespaces if provided
 	if v, ok := args["namespaces"].(string); ok && v != "" {
@@ -956,3 +963,7 @@ func (s *mcpServer) callStartKubeshark(args map[string]any) (string, bool) {
 	// Execute the command in headless mode (no browser popup)
 	cmdArgs = append(cmdArgs, "--set", "headless=true")
 
+	// Append the pod regex as a positional argument, guarded by "--" so it is
+	// always
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `--` separator before appending the `pod_regex` argument to prevent argument injection. It also adds robust path validation and confinement for the `download_file` function, ensuring that destination paths are resolved securely within a designated download directory and preventing path traversal via `../` segments or symlinks.

## GHSA-259w-wmqg-fp76 · termux-sync

2026-11-16 · Python · **HIGH** 7.1 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_WRITE`

### Root cause

The `termux-sync` tool, during its restore operation, used an attacker-controlled 'source' path from the manifest to determine the extraction destination for archive members. This allowed an attacker to specify arbitrary paths, including absolute paths or paths containing '..' components, leading to files being written outside the intended backup directory.

### Impact

An attacker could write arbitrary files to any location on the file system where the `termux-sync` process has write permissions. This could lead to overwriting critical system files, injecting malicious scripts, or achieving remote code execution.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/termux_sync/__init__.py
+++ b/src/termux_sync/__init__.py
@@ -277,18 +277,62 @@ def _relock():
     return out
 
 
+def _safe_extract_member(member: tarfile.TarInfo, dest_path: Path):
+    dest_root = dest_path.resolve()
+
+    member_name = member.name
+    if member_name.startswith("/") or member_name.startswith("\\"):
+        raise ValueError(f"Unsafe absolute path in archive: {member_name!r}")
+    if any(part == ".." for part in Path(member_name).parts):
+        raise ValueError(f"Path traversal attempt in archive: {member_name!r}")
+
+    target = (dest_path / member_name).resolve()
+    try:
+        target.relative_to(dest_root)
+    except ValueError:
+        raise ValueError(f"Archive member escapes destination: {member_name!r} -> {target}")
+
+    if member.issym() or member.islnk():
+        link_target = member.linkname
+        if os.path.isabs(link_target):
+            raise ValueError(f"Unsafe absolute link target in archive: {member_name!r} -> {link_target!r}")
+        resolved_link = (target.parent / link_target).resolve()
+        try:
+            resolved_link.relative_to(dest_root)
+        except ValueError:
+            raise ValueError(f"Archive link escapes destination: {member_name!r} -> {link_target!r}")
+
+    if member.ischr() or member.isblk() or member.isfifo():
+        raise ValueError(f"Refusing device/fifo entry in archive: {member_name!r}")
+
+
 def extract_archive(archive_path: Path, dest_path: Path, label: str, progress: Progress):
     task = progress.add_task(f"[cyan]{label}", total=None)
     dest_path.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
+
+    has_data_filter = hasattr(tarfile, "data_filter")
+
     with tarfile.open(archive_path, "r:*") as tf:
         for member in tf.getmembers():
             try:
-                tf.extract(member, dest_path, set_attrs=True)
+                _safe_extract_member(member, dest_path)
             except ValueError as e:
                 log("WARNING", f"Skipped unsafe archive member during restore: {e}")
                 continue
+
+            try:
+                if has_data_filter:
+                    tf.extract(member, dest_path, set_attrs=True, filter="data")
+                else:
+                    tf.extract(member, dest_path, set_attrs=True)
             except Exception:
                 try:
                     member.uid = os.getuid()
                     member.gid = os.getgid()
-                    tf.extract(member, dest_path, set_attrs=False)
+                    if has_data_filter:
+                        tf.extract(member, dest_path, set_attrs=False, filter="data")
+                    else:
+                        tf.extract(member, dest_path, set_attrs=False)
                 except Exception:
                     pass
     progress.update(task, total=1, completed=1, description=f"[green]{label} ✓")
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `_safe_extract_member` function to validate archive member paths, preventing absolute paths, path traversal, and symlink/hardlink escapes. It also ensures that the restore destination `src_dir` is derived only from a predefined list of trusted `BACKUP_ITEMS` and not from the attacker-controlled manifest 'source' field.

## GHSA-rg72-5958-c65m · silverstripe/framework

2026-11-16 · PHP · **MODERATE** 6.8 · Pattern: `PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION→ROLE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an insufficient check in the `canEdit` method for `Group` objects. A user with `CMS_ACCESS_SecurityAdmin` permission could edit groups that did not directly have the 'ADMIN' code, even if those groups inherited 'ADMIN' or other privileged permissions from parent groups or roles. This allowed them to assign themselves to an administrator-equivalent group.

### Impact

An attacker with `CMS_ACCESS_SecurityAdmin` permissions could escalate their privileges to full administrator access within the Silverstripe CMS, gaining control over the entire system.

### Key diff

```
-	!Permission::get()->filter(['GroupID' => $this->ID, 'Code' => 'ADMIN'])->exists()
+	!$this->grantsEscalatingPermissions()
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces new helper methods, `grantsEscalatingPermissions()` and `anyGroupGrantsPermission()`, to comprehensively check if a group, or any of its ancestors, grants any permission that could lead to 'ADMIN' access. The `canEdit` method now uses these new checks to prevent non-admin users from editing groups that would grant escalating privileges.

## GHSA-24mq-4vc6-2prf · mail-parser

2026-11-16 · Python · **MODERATE** 6.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The mail-parser library was vulnerable to several issues related to how it processed attacker-controlled email headers. Specifically, it performed linear scans for header lookups, leading to quadratic time complexity (CWE-407) with many distinct header names. Additionally, it allowed sender-chosen header names to shadow internal attributes or trigger recursive processing (CWE-407, CWE-436), leading to TypeErrors, stack exhaustion, or excessive memory allocation due to repeated serialization (CWE-405).

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious email that, when parsed, would cause the application to consume excessive CPU time, memory, or crash due to stack exhaustion, leading to a denial of service for the parsing process or the entire application.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/mailparser/core.py
+++ b/src/mailparser/core.py
@@ -296,6 +317,96 @@ def _reset(self):
         self._defects = []
         self._defects_categories = set()
         self._has_defects = False
+        self._mail = {}
+        self._mail_partial = {}
+        self._header_index = self._build_header_index()
+
+    def _build_header_index(self):
+        index = {}
+        if self.message:
+            for name, value in self.message.items():
+                index.setdefault(name.lower(), []).append(value)
+        return index
+
+    def _header_value(self, name):
+        name_header = name.lower()
+
+        if name_header in ADDRESSES_HEADERS:
+            values = self._header_index.get(name_header)
+            raw_header = values[0] if values else ""
+            parsed_addresses = get_addresses(raw_header)
+            return [
+                (
+                    (
+                        ""
+                        if (decoded_name := decode_header_part(name)) == email_addr
+                        else decoded_name
+                    ),
+                    email_addr,
+                )
+                for name, email_addr in parsed_addresses
+                if email_addr
+            ]
+
+        return decode_headers(self._header_index.get(name_header))
 
     def _append_defects(self, part, part_content_type):
         """
@@ -342,7 +453,15 @@ def _make_mail(self, complete=True):
 
         for i in keys:
             log.debug(f"Getting header or part {i!r}")
-            value = getattr(self, i)
+            if i in _RESERVED_MAIL_KEYS:
+                continue
+            value = getattr(self, i) if i in COMPUTED_PARTS else self._header_value(i)
             if value:
                 mail[i] = value
 
@@ -362,11 +481,18 @@ def parse(self):
             Instance of MailParser with raw email parsed
         """
 
-        if not self.message:
+        self._reset()
+
+        if self.message is None:
             return self
 
-        # reset and start parsing
-        self.
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a header index to optimize header lookups from O(distinct x total) to O(1) by pre-building a lowercased name to raw values map. It also prevents sender-chosen header names from shadowing internal attributes or triggering unintended recursive processing by explicitly checking against a list of reserved keys and using a dedicated `_header_value` method that avoids attribute lookups and name rewriting for external headers.

## GHSA-7h45-q5jx-7r87 · directus

2026-11-16 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 6.5 · Pattern: `PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION→ROLE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from insufficient access control checks when updating 'directus_shares' items. Specifically, users could modify the 'user_created' field to reassign ownership or the 'role' field to change the associated role, bypassing intended permissions.

### Impact

An attacker could reassign shares to other users, potentially gaining access to data they shouldn't have, or modify the role associated with a share, leading to unauthorized privilege escalation.

### Key diff

```
--- a/api/src/services/shares.ts
+++ b/api/src/services/shares.ts
@@ -46,6 +46,41 @@ export class SharesService extends ItemsService {
 	}
 
 	override async updateMany(keys: PrimaryKey[], data: Partial<Item>, opts?: MutationOptions): Promise<PrimaryKey[]> {
+		if ('user_created' in data) {
+			throw new InvalidPayloadError({ reason: `You can't change the "user_created" value manually` });
+		}
+
+--- a/packages/system-data/src/app-access-permissions/index.ts
+++ b/packages/system-data/src/app-access-permissions/index.ts
@@ -203,7 +203,7 @@ export const appRecommendedPermissions: Partial<Permission>[] = [
 				_eq: '$CURRENT_USER',
 			},
 		},
-		fields: ['*'],
+		fields: ['name', 'collection', 'item', 'role', 'password', 'date_start', 'date_end', 'max_uses', 'times_used'],
 	},
 	{
 		collection: 'directus_shares',
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces explicit checks in the `SharesService.updateMany` method to prevent manual changes to the 'user_created' field. It also restricts the fields that can be updated for 'directus_shares' by non-admin users, preventing modification of sensitive fields like 'role', and adds a `validateAccess` call for item/collection changes.

## GHSA-qqrg-8jpm-8mph · silverstripe/framework

2026-11-16 · PHP · **MODERATE** 5.4 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The XSS sanitiser in Silverstripe Framework did not adequately block data URIs that could be interpreted as scriptable documents, specifically XHTML, XML, and application/xml types. While `data:text/html` was blocked, other XML-based MIME types that could render inline scripts were overlooked, allowing attackers to bypass the sanitization.

### Impact

An attacker could inject malicious scripts into the application, leading to client-side attacks such as session hijacking, defacement, or redirection to malicious sites.

### Key diff

```
-            $this->splitWithWhitespaceRegex('data:text/html'),
+            $this->splitWithWhitespaceRegex('data:text/html'),
+            $this->splitWithWhitespaceRegex('data:application/xhtml+xml'),
+            $this->splitWithWhitespaceRegex('data:text/xml'),
+            $this->splitWithWhitespaceRegex('data:application/xml'),
```

### Fix summary

The patch extends the XSS sanitization regex to include additional data URI MIME types (`data:application/xhtml+xml`, `data:text/xml`, `data:application/xml`) that can execute JavaScript. It also ensures that special characters within the MIME types are properly escaped in the regex to prevent unintended matching.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 158 occurrences**
> Languages: Python, JavaScript, PHP, Java, Go, RUST, Ruby

## GHSA-xwg4-8hqh-79jq · silverstripe/framework

2026-11-16 · PHP · **MODERATE** 5.4 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The XSS sanitiser in Silverstripe Framework did not adequately filter SVG data URIs. Specifically, it missed `data:image/svg` variations, allowing attackers to embed malicious scripts within SVG images that would execute when rendered in a user's browser.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary client-side scripts into web pages, leading to session hijacking, defacement, or redirection to malicious sites.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Core/XssSanitiser.php
+++ b/src/Core/XssSanitiser.php
@@ -224,6 +225,12 @@ private function getStripAttributeContentsRegex(): string
             $this->splitWithWhitespaceRegex('data:application/xml'),
             $this->splitWithWhitespaceRegex('vbscript:'),
         ];
+        if ($this->removeDataSvg) {
+            // SVG documents can carry scripts, so an SVG data URI is a script-capable document reference.
+            // Note the media type is matched as a prefix so that `data:image/svg+xml` and any other
+            // `data:image/svg*` variation are both caught.
+            $regexes[] = $this->splitWithWhitespaceRegex('data:image/svg');
+        }
         // Regex is "starts with any of these, with optional whitespace at the start, case insensitive"
         // \x08 is the backspace character, though it only causes vulnerabilities if it's at the start
         // or end of a string
```

### Fix summary

The patch enhances the XSS sanitiser by adding a new regex rule to explicitly remove `data:image/svg` URIs from HTML attributes. This prevents the embedding of script-capable SVG documents.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 158 occurrences**

## GHSA-3qh3-54qg-gpq3 · silverstripe/framework

2026-11-16 · PHP · **MODERATE** 4.3 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The GridFieldDetailForm_ItemRequest class, responsible for handling item creation and editing in GridField, did not properly check 'canCreate' permissions when creating new records. This allowed users to bypass the intended access controls for creating new data objects.

### Impact

An attacker could create new records of any data object type managed by GridField, even if they lacked the necessary permissions, leading to unauthorized data creation or manipulation.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Forms/GridField/GridFieldDetailForm_ItemRequest.php
+++ b/src/Forms/GridField/GridFieldDetailForm_ItemRequest.php
@@ -821,6 +821,17 @@ public function httpError($errorCode, $errorMessage = null)
      */
     protected function saveFormIntoRecord($data, $form)
     {
+        if (!$this->record->ID
+            && (!$this->record->hasMethod('canCreate') || !$this->record->canCreate(null, $this->getCreateContext()))
+        ) {
+            $this->httpError(403, _t(
+                __CLASS__ . '.CreatePermissionsFailure',
+                'It seems you don\'t have the necessary permissions to create "{ObjectTitle}"',
+                ['ObjectTitle' => $this->getModelName()]
+            ));
+            return null;
+        }
+
         $list = $this->gridField->getList();
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds a new permission check at the beginning of the `saveFormIntoRecord` method. It verifies that if a new record is being created (i.e., `record->ID` is null), the current user has the `canCreate` permission for that specific record type, preventing unauthorized creation.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 96 occurrences**

---

## GHSA-5rmc-48h2-hvcm · aiohttp

2026-11-16 · Python · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The WebSocket `_size` queue limit was not properly enforced for small or compressed messages. Only the raw message size was accounted for, ignoring the overhead associated with each message object in the queue. This allowed an attacker to send many small messages, causing the queue to exceed its intended memory limit without triggering the pause mechanism.

### Impact

An attacker could send a large number of small WebSocket messages, leading to excessive memory consumption and potentially a denial of service for the server or client application due to resource exhaustion.

### Key diff

```
--- a/aiohttp/_websocket/reader_py.py
+++ b/aiohttp/_websocket/reader_py.py
@@ -110,7 +110,7 @@ def feed_data(
     def feed_data(
         self, data: "WSMessage | WSMessageTextBytes", size: "cython_int"
     ) -> None:
-        self._size += size
+        self._size += size + MSG_SIZE_OVERHEAD
         self._put_buffer((data, size))
         self._release_waiter()
         if self._size > self._limit and not self._protocol._reading_paused:
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `MSG_SIZE_OVERHEAD` constant (128 bytes) which is added to the reported size of each message when it's fed into the queue. This ensures that the memory overhead of each message object is accounted for, preventing the queue from exceeding its configured limit with many small messages. It also refines the logic for pausing and resuming reading to better manage the queue's state.

## GHSA-jm3w-gw2p-xjfj · aiohttp

2026-11-16 · Python · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The original implementation of `MaskDomain` used `re.compile` with a manually constructed regex pattern for wildcard matching in domain names. When a specially crafted malicious domain string containing many '*' characters was passed, the regex compilation or matching process could become extremely slow, leading to an event loop stall.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious domain string that, when processed by the `add_domain()` function, would cause the aiohttp event loop to stall indefinitely, effectively leading to a denial of service for the application.

### Key diff

```
--- a/aiohttp/web_urldispatcher.py
+++ b/aiohttp/web_urldispatcher.py
-        mask = self._domain.replace(".", r"\.").replace("*", "[^:]*") + "(:.*)?"
-        self._mask = re.compile(mask, re.I)
+        host_mask, _, self._port = self._domain.partition(":")
+        self._mask = re.compile(fnmatch.translate(host_mask), re.I)
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `MaskDomain` class to use `fnmatch.translate` for converting host masks into regular expressions, which is more efficient and safer for wildcard matching. It also separates the host and port matching logic to prevent the port part from interfering with the host wildcard matching.

## GHSA-jq3q-62jc-fqq9 · code-review-mcp-server

2026-11-16 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The application constructed shell commands by directly concatenating user-supplied input (like folderPath, baseBranch, branchName, filePath) into `execSync` calls. This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary shell commands by crafting malicious input that would break out of the intended command structure.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary commands on the server where the application is running, leading to full system compromise, data exfiltration, or denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/utils/getGitHandler.ts
+++ b/src/utils/getGitHandler.ts
@@ -1,4 +1,4 @@
-import { execSync } from 'child_process';
+import { execFileSync } from 'child_process';
 import {
   type FileChange,
   getIgnorePatterns,
@@ -14,8 +14,9 @@ const getFileChanges = (
   baseBranch: string,
   currentBranch: string,
 ): FileChange[] => {
-  const numstatOutput = execSync(
-    `git -C "${folderPath}" diff --numstat ${baseBranch}..${currentBranch}`,
+  const numstatOutput = execFileSync(
+    'git',
+    ['-C', folderPath, 'diff', '--numstat', `${baseBranch}..${currentBranch}`],
     { encoding: 'utf-8' },
   ).trim();
```

### Fix summary

The patch replaces `execSync` with `execFileSync` for all external command executions. `execFileSync` takes the command and its arguments as separate parameters, preventing shell metacharacters in user input from being interpreted as commands.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 143 occurrences**
> Languages: JavaScript, Python, Java, PHP, Go, Ruby, YAML, RUST, C#

## GHSA-pw86-h8fm-j323 · aiohttp

2026-11-16 · Python · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

During an HTTP upgrade request (e.g., WebSocket), the aiohttp server would buffer all incoming data into `_message_tail` without any size limit. This occurred because the mechanism to pause reading from the transport was not applied to the `_message_tail` buffer, only to the `_messages` queue for pipelined requests.

### Impact

An attacker could send an arbitrarily large amount of data after an upgrade request, causing the server to consume excessive memory and potentially crash or become unresponsive, leading to a denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/aiohttp/web_protocol.py
+++ b/aiohttp/web_protocol.py
@@ -462,6 +467,11 @@ def data_received(self, data: bytes) -> None:
         # no parser, just store
         elif self._payload_parser is None and self._upgraded and data:
             self._message_tail += data
+            if (
+                not self._msg_queue_paused
+                and len(self._message_tail) >= self._read_bufsize
+            ):
+                self._pause_msg_queue_reading()
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `_read_bufsize` limit for the `_message_tail` buffer during upgrade requests. When the buffered data exceeds this limit, the transport reading is paused. It also ensures that reading is resumed once the buffered data is processed or if the queue drains.

## GHSA-xgw8-8ccx-rmqh · aiohttp

2026-11-16 · Python · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The aiohttp web server did not limit the number of form fields processed from URL-encoded or multipart/form-data requests. An attacker could send a request with an excessively large number of form fields, causing the server to spend a significant amount of time parsing them, thus blocking the event loop and consuming CPU resources.

### Impact

An attacker could perform a denial-of-service (DoS) attack by sending a crafted request with many form fields, making the server unresponsive to legitimate requests and potentially leading to service unavailability.

### Key diff

```
--- a/aiohttp/web_request.py
+++ b/aiohttp/web_request.py
@@ -180,6 +186,7 @@ def __init__(
         loop: asyncio.AbstractEventLoop,
         *,
         client_max_size: int = 1024**2,
+        client_max_fields: int = 1000,
         state: dict[RequestKey[Any] | str, Any] | None = None,
         scheme: str | None = None,
         host: str | None = None,
@@ -887,17 +897,22 @@ async def post(self) -> "MultiDictProxy[_Post]":
                     raise ValueError(
                         "To decode nested multipart you need to use custom reader",
                     )
+        elif not (data := await self.read()):
+            out = MultiDict()
         else:
-            data = await self.read()
-            if data:
-                charset = self.charset or "utf-8"
-                out.extend(
-                    parse_qsl(
-                        data.rstrip().decode(charset),
-                        keep_blank_values=True,
+            charset = self.charset or "utf-8"
+            query = data.rstrip().decode(charset)
+            max_fields = self._client_max_fields
+            try:
+                out = MultiDict(
+                    query_to_pairs(
+                        query,
+                        max_fields=max_fields if max_fields > 0 else None,
                         encoding=charset,
                     )
                 )
+            except ValueError:
+                raise _too_many_fields(max_fields) from None
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new configuration option, `client_max_fields`, which defaults to 1000. This limit is enforced during the parsing of URL-encoded and multipart/form-data requests. If the number of fields exceeds this limit, an `HTTPRequestEntityTooLarge` exception is raised, preventing resource exhaustion.
